[[Note: The following table of contents is not the final text because rationalisation and editorial corrections are pending]] We enter LEGISLATIVE WORK Only debate and vote on the principle, articles and all of the draft law of the Ministry of Civil Protection: "National Mechanism for Crisis Management and Risk Management, Restructuring of the General Secretariat of Civil Protection, Upgrading of a System of Voluntary Civil Protection, Reorganisation of the Fire Corps and other provisions". The Conference of Presidents decided at its meeting on 30 January 2020 to discuss the draft law at a single meeting on the principle, the articles and the amendment. Does the House agree?
ParlaMint-GR_2020-02-04-S1-commons.u2	The House has agreed. Please open the registration system. The rapporteur of the New Democracy, Mr George Kotrias, has the floor for 15 minutes.
ParlaMint-GR_2020-02-04-S1-commons.u3	Mr President, Minister, ladies and gentlemen, this draft law entitled 'National Mechanism for Crisis Management and Risk Management, the restructuring of the General Secretariat for Civil Protection, the upgrading of a system for civil protection volunteering, the reorganisation of the Fire Corps and other provisions' comes at a time when, firstly, climate change is manifested with more frequent and fierce weather patterns testing to our infrastructure, secondly, the arbitrary human activity that has become almost a norm multiplier of the risks and effects of any possible natural disaster, thirdly, scientific and technological data and international experience are progressing rapidly by giving new outlets and new tools to the fight against natural hazards, fourthly, it is proving failure by the application of the existing framework, which has not only protected, but has collapsed and carried away from the loss of human life. Ladies and gentlemen, this bill is the answers to all of this, it is the fulfilment of the obligation of the political system to create a modern, effective, institutional framework for protecting our country's life and property and natural environment. This constitutional obligation of the state comes today the Government of New Democracy boldly and politically bravely to implement with this bill what comes to a vote from the Greek Parliament, a bill well-processed, well-processed in every aspect of it, working with a strong and experienced staff, a bill that, after public consultation and constructive discussion, comes to four meetings of the Committees of Government and the Environment today in plenary and aspires to benefit from the widest possible confidence since it lays the foundations from the very beginning and builds the construction of civil protection and at the same time builds on new solid foundations and upgrades the operation of the Firehouse that brings it to the main arm of civil protection for our country. If we look back historically, we will see that the first framework entitled "Political Protection Organization" appears in 1995, supplemented in 2002, after 2003 and 2006, and with Law 4249/2014 it was attempted to modernise it, but without ever being achieved, since a series of implementing and regulatory decrees were never adopted and the country suffered one damage after another with a common feature not finally knowing who gives the orders, who has the responsibility for decision-making, what the structure of decision-making is, and in the end we had the phenomena of disseminating responsibilities, since one was blamed on the other and paid for it. You do not need to be an expert to see that, in particular, the lack of coordination, structural contagion and operational weaknesses have made the law of 2002 obsolete and unable to respond to the current climatic, technological and social conditions, which unfortunately has proved to be the case with disastrous results. If, therefore, we agree on the need for a new, modern and effective institutional framework for civil protection, then I believe that unanimously we will agree that this draft law is an intrinsic part of crisis management, first of all, the prevention and response of risks, is innovating the immediateness of interventions and preventive interventions and with its provisions, making use of the voluntary movement, reorganising the structure and functioning of the Firehouse, which, as I said before, is reflected in the main lever of civil protection. Ladies and gentlemen, it is first because it responds exactly to all the problems of the existing legal framework, establishing a vertical structure with clear competences, so that instead of the division and dissemination of irresponsible-responsible-responsible-responsible-responsible-responsible-responsible-responsible-responsible-responsible-responsible-responsible-responsibility, we can now know who is doing what, who controls who is doing it and who is responsible for what has been done it. And this is done with a structure by the national commander and thirteen regional governors, people of experts and acquainted with civil protection procedures. I would like to stress that this is not just another civil protection bill, but it is about creating a national mechanism. In fact, ladies and gentlemen, for the first time we have created a National Mechanism for Crisis Management and Risk Management with a fully vertically integrated structure in the US FEMA and Russian EMERCOM model and clear competences for all levels of administration, namely the central one, after the regional one and, thirdly, for the local level by ensuring shorter decision-making, reaction, the elimination of bureaucracy, but also the diffusion regime and, ultimately, the withdrawal of responsibilities. Article 11 provides for the coordinating bodies of civil protection. Article 12 defines the maximum coordination body. Article 13 recommends regional civil protection coordination bodies, which operate continuously and continuously, to be established at regional level by decision-making - take care - of the regional director. In addition to the regional and regional civil protection coordinator, as defined by the district commander, the spatial and thematic counter-regions, the senior guard commander, the fire commander, the regional police director, the port commander and the health officer shall be involved. At the same time, Article 14 sets out the responsibilities of these regional civil protection coordination bodies. I mention, for example, that they are following the design of the regional emergency management frameworks, the WFPs, they are planning, organising and implementing actions to inform and notify citizens and others. I referred to them, because all those who, at the meetings of the committees, were questioned whether or not they were involved, that is, the regional directors, the regional directors, the thematic vice-presidents and others, seem to be actively involved in the system in clear and clear terms. But do you know what's changing? We will now know that at the time of the crisis we will not desperately look for a political person on the phone, but one, the responsible coordinator, the regional coordinator of civil protection. The same clear involvement and involvement provides for the bill with Article 15 for each municipality of Greece, which is set up locally operational, coordination civil protection bodies, with clear responsibilities, which are created by the mayor and which participate, apart from the mayor who presides and the vice mayor, the Head of the independent civil protection section, and all the local actors involved, not to mention them again. The three levels of crisis management, which are vertically integrated, have direct interdependence and vertical structure, as the local helps the regional community and regional helps the central coordination centre. It is worth staying here a little further in local government and I say that the actions that are being organised and implemented by the mayors and local coordination centres are mentioned in Article 16. Also, the involvement of self-government is described in Articles 19, 20, 21, entitled "Supporting services of National Mechanism" and I distinguish the paragraph (e) of Article 20 and the paragraph (e) of Article 21, which provides that the registrant and the mayor respectively are responsible for drawing up within eighteen months, with their sole responsibility, an emergency plan for which on a monthly basis the mayor will report to the regional coordinator, with an updated list of human resources and resources. Because, ladies and gentlemen, those who have been mayors know very well what it is to snow suddenly and unforeseenly and to find employees at the peak of the phenomenon. Apart from crisis management, however, the second major innovation in this bill is efficiency in risk prevention. The draft law meets the three conditions for effective prevention: firstly, the existence of a mechanism for recording and studying risks; secondly, the existence of a mechanism and flexible financing; and thirdly, the possibility of a rapid delegation and implementation of urgent measures. For the first time, a National Database of Risks and Threats, Loss, Destroyance, is established with Articles 22 and 23. And not only is there a register of Risk Records established, but the claim of European funding is also strengthened, since the existence of this national basis is a criterion for future European funding. Secondly, emergency management frameworks are being established, the MIPs to which I referred earlier, with Article 10. Thirdly, it is established under Article 40 Crisis Management Study Centre, the so-called CEMIC. Fourthly, for the first time, under Article 26 and its innovative regulation, areas threatened with special mobilisation may be declared. It is for the first time foreseen under Article 42 ‘fast track’ procedures for the rapid licensing and the execution of emergency small-scale and preventive projects, and in fact within ninety days, since they are, of course, assessed by the Risk Assessment Committee, and an Economic Services Directorate is set up under Articles 44 to 52, which is essential for managing the funds of the upgraded civil protection policy. With regard to the rapid award and implementation of projects, it is recommended under Article 37 that the National Civil Protection Agency, as well as Article 38, Operational Risk Prevention and Management Fund, the so-called ERDF, should be established. However, in addition to the prevention and management of crises, in which, of course, the bill is first and foremost and fundamentally altering philosophy, the draft law can also be taken into account, and the exploitation and management of the intellectual potential, first of all by replacing the non-functional regulatory framework of volunteers, with a clear set of operational involvement rules, with regard to the volunteering of civil protection, with emphasis on the protection of volunteers, their education, and with a clear categorisation of voluntary organisations, which can now be strengthened by materials and instruments to better integrate them into the National Civil Protection Mechanism. But since we are talking about intellectual potential, above all we are talking about the Fire Corps, which is upgraded through the bill and made with the Greek Police a fundamental pillar of the protection of the life and property of Greek citizens. The upgrade starts with the education, training and training of the Fire Department officials, the reform of the Fire Academy and the establishment of the National Crisis Management School, which is first structured at the Fire Academy, secondly at the Academy of Civil Protection and thirdly at the Centre of Special Training. In parallel to the training of executives and the integration of the system of introduction into the pan-Hellenic exams, the bill goes on to decentralisation, with the creation of new decentralised regional administrations, with specific local competences and administrative and operational autonomy. A firefighting industry and administrative support branch is established, subject to the relevant addresses, departments and offices. The bill upgrades a large part of the fire workers, graduates and graduates, with the introduction of staff posts of special tasks, engineers, economists, law. However, the upgrading of the operation of the Fire Corps is also going through something else. It passes through a pioneering evaluation system of officers, through a rated rated rating table, impartial, transparent and worthy, but not only of officers but also of low-grade fire personnel, restoring existing inequalities. And all this is done on the basis of measurable elements of professional competence, so that the strains of the Fire Corps operate in a level playing field and are judged by their personal performance alone. The same reasoning prevailed in the reform of the provisions on transfers, for which the adoption of a new, technically sound transfer regulation is now envisaged, based on mobilisation for all, ensuring a better distribution of staff in the territory, as well as an exemption from the logic of customer and ruptured logic. The draft law, other than the permanent ones, shall also regulate five-year obligation issues, since Article 180 deletes the concept of potential renewal and expressly provides that the contract is renewed under the terms and conditions of paragraph 7. In addition, paragraph 2 of 180 eliminates the age limit, unregulated transfer and distraction issues are currently regulated, gives retrospectiveness to the molecules of transfers to those who have been permanently permanently recruited, gives the possibility to re-entry for the next four years of those who have been employed in 2019. Ladies and gentlemen, since I heard the bodies, I have made sure that everyone finds that the bill is in a positive direction and that some bodies, perhaps common to the current logic of their involvement in the existing system, want to continue a similar system, giving their participation a greater role, regardless of whether or not this participation would be more beneficial to the end result. It is clear that this draft law creates another kind of structure, it actually creates a national mechanism, a strong General Secretariat for Civil Protection and a mechanism for direct and effective response, both at operational level and at the level of preventive intervention and financing of construction and infrastructure. The new law, with its respective decrees and decisions, which will be adopted as soon as possible, will certainly be a possible weapon for Greek society in the battle against any kind of crisis of physical or technological destruction, especially now that all scientists worldwide warn us that in the coming years it will be very difficult in terms of the effects of climate change. Ladies and gentlemen, by analyzing and studying one - one of the articles in the bill and invoking my multiannual experience in self-government, I can today tell you that I am convinced that this is an important section, a step forward in logic, mentality, but also in the structure of civil protection in the country. I am sure that this bill is the nationally required response to Mandra's unanswered 'why'. It's our contribution to the memory of the losers in Matty. It is the minimum contribution of the country's multiplely exposed political system to restoring the non-existent in recent years of people's confidence in this. Today's bill, ladies and gentlemen, is the New Democracy's contribution to building from the principle of society's lost confidence in the state. And that is why I call on you to vote in favour of it. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-02-04-S1-commons.u5	Good morning, Mr President, everyone. Today we are discussing a draft law very important. This is essentially a multi-project which is not just about civil protection with 54 articles. It concerns the restructuring essentially of the Firehouse through 108 articles, it has arrangements for voluntary organisations with 18 articles, arrangements concerning the transfer of the General Secretariat for Anti-Criminal Policy by the Ministry of Justice to the Ministry of Civil Protection, an article by the Ministry of Health, and of course, we also have an amendment on immigration tabled last night. This is a bill, which came to a New Year's Eve consultation. And that says something. This consultation lasted for about thirteen days, but without the opinion of the Standing Committee on Otto Responsibilities. I would also say that there are doubts as to whether the relevant ministries have been consulted. For twelve ministries sign this draft law. What is civil protection? Based on Law 3013/2002, the State is operating to protect the life, health and property of its citizens, to protect against natural and other disasters causing emergency situations during a peaceful period, as well as to care for material or cultural goods, wealthy sources, infrastructurals of the country, with a view to minimising the consequences and disasters. It is based on a quadruple risk recognition, preparation - prevention, impact mitigation - preparedness - treatment, short recovery. Civil protection is a national matter, ladies and gentlemen. It is a matter of many services, private and public bodies, but also of all citizens. A modern model of civil protection is therefore required to respond to three main questions: the successful coordination of actions, the decision-making process - who decides - elimination of bureaucracy. And all this, especially after the events and disasters we had and experienced a year ago in Matti, Mandra, Halkidiki, Evia and other regions, makes it necessary to restructure the system. The problem of civil protection is the non-horizontal interconnection of a mechanism and not the creation of new administrative structures. There must be decentralised civil protection instruments and services must communicate with each other. Today we are experiencing the era of the climate crisis, a global issue. The world community is concerned, scientists clearly warn, extreme weather phenomena grow in number, but also fierceness. So natural and environmental disaster management policies must incorporate policies to prevent and tackle the consequences of climate change. This is necessary because, it seems, human life costs will be increased geometrically, while damage recovery costs will be increased exponentially. That is why we also need a modern, efficient and restructured civil protection system in our country. SYRIZA in October 2019 submitted a draft law that was established - we did not have time to vote when SYRIZA was a government - but this draft law came after a significant consultation with positive comments from the CEDEFOP, the European Civil Protection Mechanism, the Standing Committee on OTA Responsibilities Control, while incorporating the scientific documentation and adopting the most modern and productive elements from Europe, the United States and Canada. We were talking about a single emergency body with a central idea of unifying all the available scientific and human resources associated with the political decision to prevent both the business and the rehabilitation with a commander, which will be a person of knowledge and competence and with a choice with specific criteria and an international competition. With a board of scientists, where Dimitrios, HMY, the National Observatory, the Hellenic Space Agency will be involved for natural disasters. With a permanent command officer from the Firefighting, the EKA, the Police, GEN, the GSP, the Forest Services. Using and renewing the resources and personnel of the Armed Forces, taking into account volunteers. Enhanced by the institution of command - incident. In order to finance this programme, the European Commission has made moves and contacts even with the European Investment Bank, through the ESRB's resources. On the other hand, the draft law which has been tabled and which we are debating today in the House only answers the main questions and questions. We have said to the committee that no issues are solved. Neither the experience of natural disasters in the country nor the international experience is considered. This draft law is dysfunctional, dysfunctional, multi-teaching and creates a new bureaucracy. It is also rough and contradictory. I shall mention one feature: Article 3 and Article 28. In Article 3, the national mechanism shall be supervised by the Secretary-General for Civil Protection and in Article 28 the General Secretariat for Civil Protection shall constitute an overriding operational structure of the national mechanism. One understands the contradiction and curiosity. We note that civil protection is not an operational body. The main operational arm is the Fire Corps. I'm coming to Article 10. The General Secretariat may adopt decisions involving organic units of ministries, regions, regional units and municipalities and confer powers. ParlaMint-GR_2020-02-04-S1-commons.u9	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, the concept of civil protection presents strong similarities and analogies to the state of war. In both cases a social formation is called upon to defend itself against a disaster which threatens the most vital, collectively, but also individual goods, human life, health, the natural environment, cultural wealth, property and so on. In both cases an organised state structures its defence in four stages: prevention, preparedness, response and rehabilitation. The fundamental feature in both emergencies is the single command, when we are in particular in the response stage, that is, when the crisis has emerged openly. Then, just as in the war, in civil protection the state of emergency asks a general. The worst thing that can happen in such a situation is the diffusion of responsibility. Following the painful experiences in Mandra and Matti, ladies and gentlemen, we are all aware that major natural disasters are usually unfolding at the level of the self-government region. It will therefore be extremely helpful to formulate a global crisis on the bill to see how it integrates the self-government regions into the mechanism of civil protection. The answer, as we have repeatedly been able to highlight in the parliamentary committee, is unfortunately negative, because at the specific level of administration, where the tragic experiences have shown us that the roles must be clear, that is exactly where the bill is confusing as it creates parallel structures. What do I mean? The regional director shall be assisted, as reasonable is, by his services, in particular by the independent Civil Protection Directorate provided for in Article 20 of the draft law and shall have the task of coordinating the work of civil protection by territorial authority. But, already in Law 4623/2019, thirteen regional civil protection coordinators, appointed by the district manager, had been created, but with the agreement of the Secretary-General of Civil Protection, with responsibility, as their title dictates, to coordinate. They are thirteen counties, to which, as the case may be, the regional director is assigned tasks solely to him. So the question arises here who will coordinate the coordinators. In essence, this question also begins with the memorandum presented to us by the Committee of the Regions, where it is made very discreetly, but it is clear that there is a need for full clarification and delimitation of the powers and jurisdiction of the regional coordinator, the regional director, the spatial districts and the civil protection officials of the regions. All this brings us to the confusion that has prevailed for months in the field of immigration and refugee, where the government successively appointed coordinators to the coordinators, so that after a six-year-old end the Ministry of Migration Policy would die, which she had moved to abolish immediately after the elections. And all this is the amendment that came to us last night on the responsibilities of Mr Stefani. Mr Stefanis had been banned - if we remember correctly - as the absolute sovereign in managing the refugee in a national co-ordinator position. Yesterday's amendment becomes a mere guardian of the borders. Ladies and gentlemen, I do not need to stress the importance of the country's immigration policy and the cost of all this time it has leaked. However, in the field of civil protection, as the tragic experiences in Mandra and Matti reminded us, the wrong organisational options may have not only painful but irreparable consequences. I mean losing human lives again. In any case, we have formulated our positions. The effects of climate change, as well as the asymmetrical threats that may occur at any time, require a holistic approach that dictates the coexistence of all structures and services directly to the Prime Minister, even through the creation of a ministry here with the Prime Minister. I hope that our assessments, as in the case of the refugee, are not verified, because what we see is that a Secretary-General's position is being established instead of the Ministry, who is always present and everything is being paid. What other Secretary-General, i.e. a posted official appointed by party criteria, can recruit as his alternate another poster? What other Secretary-General may decide on financing intended to cover horizontal and interministerial needs? What other Secretary-General can he hire and dismiss boards of organs set up by him, even teaching staff in schools that are equivalent to HEIs? Who else has the power to conclude with his own decision memos with private operators for the provision of services and even to set their tariffs? Who else is recommended to an Interministerial Committee? Who else is in agreement on the appointment of a poster to a self-government local authority, such as the district hereditary? It is clear that this over-ambitious project cannot function at the level of a Secretary-General. The Secretary-General shall remain a senior, but a posted official. And it is obvious that the risk of not being heard at the critical moment by key factors, political, military and service forces. While the explanatory statement mentions the need for civil protection to be redefined in the current context of threats and others and also refers to a holistic approach, in essence the 2/3 legislation concerns firefighting. Even these arrangements are incomplete, as risks should have been defined, there should be a design of structures and functions based on these risks by region, there should be a design for infrastructure projects at all stages and especially at prevention, so that there can be an operational framework for civil protection. For the first time, classified funds are established in civil protection. They are to be managed under the minimum conditions of credibility, without a representative of the judiciary, with persons designated by the executive, while, of course, they remain undetermined. Civil protection is aimed at protecting against disasters - natural and technical - to which there are no national security issues, so that there is a need for classified management of resources. As far as the Fire Corps is concerned now, the bill regulates issues of the organization of the Fire Corps, as well as issues of hierarchy, evaluation and service development and changes of fire officers. Articles 77 and 79 redistribute the organisational positions of the staff and defines the organisational structure and structure of the services. However, the creation of service structures, the allocation, the increase or reduction of positions should be based on objective criteria and operational data to ensure transparency and reliability. It is therefore a serious study that will be a prerequisite for the creation of structures and for the distribution of positions. This will tell us what the needs are at every point in the precinct and such a study, unfortunately, is not foreseen. The proposal for the rating creates two speed firefighters. For the many - nine thousand six hundred firemen, firefighters and firefighters - there are only four steps of development, which means a lack of opportunities and incentives for the vast majority of workers. For the few there are thirteen steps. There is scope for improving the development of low-level staff and further rationalisation of the rating and it is necessary to explore the possibility of a faster development, the promotion of archivists to the extent of a firearm in twenty years of total service and not twenty-six. There are also non-productive firefighters. Perhaps they will enter the Archipelagos School without exams as supernumerals. Why? Because there is a need to strengthen the profile of the Fire Corps with qualified executives. There's for the firearms that need to be corrected. It is necessary to add to Article 181, to the ‘specific matters’, a transitional provision providing that the provisions of Article 114(4) apply also to pyrodics who at the time of entry into force of this law have eight years to the extent of them. The need for this provision lies in the fact that the wording of Article 184 excludes the possibility of promoting to the extent of the anti-missile a significant number of firearms carrying that figure not three years but eight years, and at the same time the possibility of these three years, provided that they have twenty-six years of total service. This inequality will inevitably lead to aggregation of court proceedings due to an unnecessarily overthrowing of the hierarchy. In the way we are proposing, it ensures the possibility of promoting the level of firefighting to both firefighting officials who have many years of total service, and to those who have, to date, been hierarchically superior to them, as they have been serving many years to the level of the firearm. This legislative initiative on civil protection, volunteering and restructuring the Fire Corps can appear as innovative, that it is allegedly guaranteeing the safety of human life, natural wealth and cultural heritage, but in practice, in the view of the JRC, it undermines it and, indeed, systematically. It repulses man and destroys the environment. That is why it cannot deal with climate change. That is why the advanced states in the European Union and around the world - Spain, America, Australia, Portugal - cannot avoid major disasters and victims. Converting socially into individual responsibility for safeguarding even the fundamental needs of the residential workers is the cause of the earthquake-driven houses, the unacceptable conditions for the residence of millions of workers, for the victims of the floods. The inappropriate need for capital to exploit the land, human resources, forests, beaches, at times with tourist accommodation, sometimes with wind turbines and green investments, is the real cause of the enormous consequences of uncontrolled forest fires. Whatever side one looks at, one will see that behind the consequences of the so-called "extreme weather" is the capitalist development path that sacrifices every employee's right to ensure the profit of big capital. In our country, and no government, nor one of them, has ever planned, nor has it financed measures of earthquake, anti-military and anti-nuclear protection of the people, nor measures to protect workers in the workplace, nor health infrastructure, welfare, school roofing, sport, based on the modern needs of the people. So from identifying any changes in the climate and the severe weather phenomena to using climate change from governments to justify criminal management of the phenomenon at the expense of the people, there is an abysmal leap. Climate change and extreme weather phenomena are used as a convenient idea to cover up the simplest: that the only ultimately extreme phenomenon, which defines workers' lives, is the capitalist development path itself. And because we do not live in the prehistoric history or the period when people lived in shackles of straw huts, let us stop treating the people as if you were addressing natives. The people are neither coward nor fatal nor indifference. The change of attitude required is simple: Our opponent is not the climate and the weather. This is the capitalist development path that prevents us from living as we deserve. And today is the same game. The disasters are exploited on the one hand to promote expensive physical phenomena management projects, but they are designed on the basis of profit and not to meet people's needs - and that is why they are not going to solve any popular problems - and on the other hand, to convince workers that they must put a deep hand in their pockets and pay for new green development, which is to save the planet, hiding that their real goal is the huge green peas of three billion euros. Substantial prevention, but also genuine treatment of phenomena when they happen, is ultimately incompatible with capitalist development itself and this draft law is a typical example of this deadlock of development for the working people. It serves the objectives of the development law, a large part of which must be credited to the SYRIZA who prepared it, which grants "gear and water" to the large capital in order to be able to invest wherever it wants in civil protection, on whatever terms it wants, without compromising the effects on the people and the environment, even with the lifting of any protection framework it has had to date. The draft law is still geared towards repression, disaster management and not to prevention, which it considers to be cost. It provides, in the context of the formulation of national disaster risk reduction policy, for the participation of bodies, including the private sector, associated with disaster risk management. It excludes any serious attempt, on the basis of real scientific data, to assess the impact of a new industrial installation on the environment and in conditions of normal operation and in conditions of intense and unforeseen weather conditions or in a serious harm. For the JRC such an assessment is the cornerstone of a serious policy of prevention and civil protection. The draft law provides for the privatisation - commercialisation of civil protection and prevention by direct linking the official state structure with private entities, construction of fire-safety infrastructure with a public-private partnership, compensatory provision of services and the provision of staff and means with a tariff for each hour of use to business groups operating airports, motorways, ports, networks, special facilities and others, by payment in return or in kind. Also, through the sale of educational and research services to public and private bodies and citizens, through the privatisation of the Fire Academy, through the participation and funding of business groups in research, education, fire protection certification, through the Crisis Management Study Centre and the National Crisis Management School, including the Fire Academy. It is also distinguished from a lack of criteria, principles, processes and guidelines for a number of issues, such as effective risk prevention, land use criteria, security distances between industrial and residential activities, roads of mass escape, free areas of refuge and others, an integrated risk assessment for the specific characteristics and activities of each region, population, as well as emergency plans by type of risk. It maintains the subsistence, insufficient means and infrastructure of the state services and especially the Fire Corps, Forest Service, State Structures, Hospitals, Health Centers etc. There is no real provision for the preparation, substantial information and education of the population. It closes eyes to the infrastructure necessary to prevent disasters and to enable their plans to be effectively implemented. It continues to provide reduced funding from the State budget for studies, required projects and infrastructures, for adequate anti-nuclear, anti-military and earthquake protection, weakens civil protection services, the Fire Corps, Forest Service, relevant municipalities and regions services in personnel, facilities, equipment and infrastructure. You lie that money cannot be found when at the same time for infamous sustainable development there are debts of billions in monopolistic groups or they are exempted from their taxation when four billion euros are wasted annually, when the government at the 'HRAKLIS' project reintroduced the banking system with 12 billion euros and so much more. The draft law militates the Fire Corps and maintains it in the Security Corps, contrary to the social character of its role and mission, by establishing the weaponry of firearms in interrogation officials. It integrates Political Protection into NATO's planning, with NATO's special office being set up in the General Secretariat of Civil Protection, and provides for cooperation with European Police and budgeting of the General Secretariat of Special Credentials. It also promotes the substitution of a significant part of the services of the Fire Corps by volunteering and non-governmental organisations, together with the assignment of fire protection, fire protection, fire stations and stairways to the regions and municipalities, by establishing thirteen regional civil protection centres and local civil protection coordination bodies, without ensuring the necessary personnel and resources, by charging popular families with additional taxes and fees. It broadens the flexible working relationships in the field of fire safety and the protection of local government bodies, the Fire Corps and the structures of the General Secretariat. It expands seasonality and continues recruitment with the five-year regime. It establishes unpaid overworking in permanent, five-year and seasonal. It creates minimum wages and ratings for low-grade permanent and contractors. It establishes flexibility and mobility. It abolishes the irreversibleity of firefighting officers, restoring their avengers continuously and exterminately. It applies the assessment of officials and fire services, aiming at further questioning the right of permanent and stable work, reducing staff and infrastructure and scaring workers, while referring to the call for recognition of the profession as a heavy and unhealthy profession. Ladies and gentlemen, the expectations that the government is seeking to cultivate through its promises to create an effective mechanism for preventing and limiting major disasters, protecting life and property, infrastructure and the environment are disguised in the most revealing way. The huge bureaucratic crisis management and risk management mechanism planned to be established, the referral of numerous decisions to the Secretary-General with the incredible superpowers on his face, joint ministerial decisions and presidential decrees, as well as the nature of the changes to the draft law and its targets serve the goals of the reactionary state to promote capitalist development, further increasing the risks and stepping up the absence of coordination. That is why, unfortunately, it will not be long before we have reintroduced even greater disasters and new loss of human life. The JRC has publicly submitted to the people, in conferences, events at the workplaces throughout the country, its positions for the overall planning of civil protection, for each side of natural and technological disasters, as well as for the integrated response, focusing on the interaction of all risk factors. The JRC no doubt believes that the great development of science and technology today enables us to minimize the consequences of our people's lives and property. We call on the working class, the people's families, firefighters, the working people's movement with determination to fight to satisfy their own needs, protect their lives and property. The organisation of civil protection, the treatment of natural and technological disasters at the level of structure, infrastructure, equipment, planning and management must be of exclusive concern and responsibility for the state, without NGO and unpaid work, with brave funding from the state budget and mass support with permanent staff. Only then can an integrated, substantial scientific design of combined valuation for all risks, physical and technological, education and information for the population, development of research in this direction under the responsibility of the State itself. Only then can there be integrated design of infrastructure, counter-flood protection, integrated forest management, prosthetic control and protection measures for public buildings and collecting sites for many people, substantial and continuous control of static adequacy and safety of all buildings in the event of earthquake, risk assessment, continuous controls and all necessary risk minimisation measures in technological installations. And finally, for the Fire Corps, we believe that in the current conditions it should be fully demilitarised on the basis of the service of its social mission, with responsibility for the treatment of urban and forest fires, rescues and fire safety. Rehabilitate their wage losses and ensure all pensions, wage rights for employees, safety and hygiene conditions, and their integration into heavy and unhealthy. Monitor all contractual firefighters, including five-year-old firefighters, with a stable working relationship, to meet all needs for the prevention, preservation and protection of forest ecosystems. To abolish all public-private partnership programmes and all business activities in education and research, which enhance the privatisation of sectors of the Fire Force and provide direct funding for the modernisation of infrastructure, fleet, logistics, supply of reliable personal equipment to all fire personnel, exclusively from the State budget. We stand alongside fire extinguishers who are fighting the dignity of life to have full cover and insurance, pension rights. Today they are out here giving this battle to defend their interests and rights. We stand by them and give together with all the people's movement the battle so that the Civil Protection is a key element in protecting human life, in protecting the environment. In all these words, we end up voting against this draft law. Thank you, Mr President.

Minister, Mr General, ladies and gentlemen, unfortunately, we are living in a country where damage has been done many times, we have been afflicted, we have even grieved victims because of natural disaster, earthquake, intense snowfall, a storm. After all, we all remember what happened just before the New Year's Day on the Athens-Lamia National Road because of bad weather and bad manoeuvres, when the traffic stopped on both currents due to snowfall. The result was large tails and hundreds of immobilized cars for hours with a central road axis paralyzed. This chaos caused by the disastrous combination of bad weather, mismanagement and lack of coordination on the part of the authorities was followed by the replacement by Mr Chrysochoidis, the Minister of Civil Protection, the commander of the second section of the Athens motorways for road errors and omissions of traffic, and the announcements of the political leadership of the Ministry of Transport and Infrastructure that he would do everything he could to keep the national road network open. Unfortunately, we have experienced many such scenes, especially when weather phenomena that are unobstructed in the climate of our country make their appearance and are extremist - in fact, we are giving them very imaginable names every time and I expect to see what name this weather that we expect in the next few days, with snowfall in Attica - mainly because the state mechanism appears unprepared and unable to respond with speed and adequacy. None of us can forget the tragic fire in Matti, but also all the previous ones, as well as the floods in Mandra. This fire in Eye cost more than a hundred people our lives, precisely because of the combination of an extreme weather phenomenon and insufficient coordination due to ignorance, panic or bad decision. To deal with major natural or administrative crises, there must be systematic preparation, education, but also a vigilance of the entire state mechanism, but also of citizens, following the most recent interdisciplinary approaches and exploiting, not only the expertise of specialized scientific resources, but also the empirical data, so that we can respond to the requirements of problems at every level of management and activity. Finally, at the level of coordination, a change in concepts of competences and technical dividing lines between operators is mainly necessary. In the light of all this, we have been very careful in this bill, because - it is true, at last - to set up an adequate mechanism to protect citizens effectively so that we do not have victims again, nor suffering due to bad weather and wrong manipulations. Unfortunately, however, we note at first glance that the legislation under discussion fails in advance to fulfil its objective and that for many reasons. First of all, we have another large bill of many hundreds of pages - seven hundred thirty two, if I am not mistaken, if we put the banned and all the reports - which stumbles on all the principles of good legislature, which, from the outset and on the relevant bill, is the government's euphoria. This draft law consists of a hundred and ninety articles, marrying important issues that should come separately, civil protection, the work of the Firehouse, the relocation of the Koridalus Prisons and many others, so that we do not have the time to discuss this despite the four meetings of the committee - three to be precise, because the latter is the hearing of the bodies - or the feedback from other competent committees in Parliament, which would have their own attention on matters relating to their own competences, such as the Social Affairs Committee on the part concerning the fire-fighting work. The draft law under discussion consists of four parts. The first is the recommendation of this Civil Protection Mechanism. We have the definition of service units and support services and disaster management strategy. The responsibilities and tasks of the General Secretariat for Civil Protection and the obligations of stakeholders in a natural disaster are also specified. The second, according to the explanatory statement, is intended to regulate and redefined issues relating to mission, action, conditions for inclusion and deletion, education, evaluation, etc. volunteers, voluntary organisations and NGOs. The third attempts in detail to 'enhance the efficiency and productivity of the administrative and operational functions of the Fire Corps, while the fourth includes the other provisions. This is a series of arrangements concerning the General Secretariat of Crime Policy, the relocation of Korydallos Prisoners, service assignments without a contract notice, the modification of the powers of the Citizens' Counselor and the creation of supervised drug use sites. Generally speaking, the legislation seems to attempt to build a huge bureaucratic crisis management mechanism, which is probably said by other colleagues today, I imagine we will hear it then from the meeting - it creates new problems and is exacerbating existing problems, as many institutions, business centres, directorates, councils, committees, with many overlapping responsibilities, thus compiling a multi-fashioned grid without a clear functional structure. In short, this is a recipe that is sure to cause malfunctions. And that is not all, there is nowhere to be provided for the funding required from the state budget, so that all the studies needed to create all those required projects and appropriate infrastructures for adequate anti-nuclear, anti-military and anti-Semitism protection, when the draft law itself is filled with predictions of the establishments and recommendations of new bodies, centres, councils and committees. In addition, there is nowhere to be expected to strengthen services to staff, tools and equipment. We are talking about the infrastructures of civil protection, fire-fighting, the Forest Service, the competent services of the municipalities and regions. As far as the part of the reorganisation of the Fire Corps is concerned, it is probably not just a matter of maintaining, but also of further deepening the militarisation of the structure of the body, as it provides for its preservation in the Security Body and its cooperation with Europol and NATO through the General Secretariat for Civil Protection, in complete contrast to the social character of its role and mission. I should like to point out the following briefly on the individual points of the bill. Already from the first article on the first page of the bill, which includes definitions, an expanded scope of reference is identified, which does not restrict the General Secretariat's action in disasters from a natural cause. We have a reference to the definition of risk to 'human activity' and to the definition of vulnerability to'social factors or processes'. In addition, in many parts of the legislation, there are references not only to 'natural and technological' but also'relevant' or 'other' threats or disasters, for example Articles 2, 22, 25 and elsewhere, while we consider this to be a matter that needs clear and compelling clarification from the government. At the same time, in the context of the superpowers assigned to the Secretary-General for Civil Protection, who now receives the powers of the Deputy Minister - I dare say - he is also given the opportunity, although the principal general refused it at the committee meeting - to declare areas threatened by disasters in a situation of special mobilisation of civil protection, as provided for in Article 26. In addition, it is granted flexibility in white to conclude contracts as provided for in Article 54. The truth is, the government has a strong preference for creating positions that are over-concentrating responsibilities. It was not long ago that the National Coordinator for Migration, today, was the upgrade of the Secretary-General for Civil Protection. Of course, with the amendment tabled and for which I will be a little further, we have a redefinition of the responsibilities of the National Coordinator, rather than limiting them. So continuing, it is equally incomprehensible and the provision for the creation of a "special appropriation of secret national needs" in the General Secretariat of Civil Protection, in the standards of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and other Ministries, in accordance with Article 53. We are really unable to understand what purpose such a provision serves, what costs it will be called upon to cover, especially when it comes to an area that should be fully transparent and justified, as it concerns the everyday life and life of all of us. Despite the efforts of the Secretary-General and the Minister to give clarification to the committee, we must admit that we are not convinced. We now go to Part II, which, as a whole, captures the Government’s apparent struggling effort to pass constitutional responsibilities on to voluntary organizations and non-governmental groups. Under the cloak of the relevant regulatory framework it is attempting to create, it covers the removal of its responsibilities and ultimately the assignment of competences to properties, the quasi-public society, by Articles 55 to 73. At the same time, we cannot ask ourselves why we have a draft law which, although it relates to Civil Protection, takes away the self-sufficiency of the Fire Corps, does not provide for any funding, despite the enormous needs for equipment and infrastructure, nor does it ensure that its vacancies are filled up. However, he intends to interconnect it with Europol and NATO. Although, on the one hand, it does not solve practical problems, on the other, it raises other issues within the Firehouse, linking the rating to wage developments and thus causing many personal differences between workers, with the possibility that many servants in the body may stand still in a step, which does not correspond to the years of actual service they have, creating practical servants of different speeds. As a result of transferring responsibilities from the Ministry of Justice to the Protection of Citizens, a derogation is renewed and a maximum of eight months is extended to the employment contracts of detention stores under Article 183 and seven organic posts are transferred from the Justice to the Civil Protection in 184, and to the Ministry of Citizen Protection as a whole the responsibility for the relocation of Korydallos Prisoners. At the same time, in discussing this point, we consider it necessary to clarify the provision of direct awards referred to in paragraphs 2 and 3 of Article 185, while we consider that a similar clarification is needed as far as compensatory benefits are concerned in the grades where the prisons are relocated, as provided for in 186. We would like to have the same clarification in terms of both Article 187 and Article 187, provided for directly the provision of consultancy services by the Civilian Protection when contracts of an estimated value of more than EUR 30 000 000 are to be concluded. The redefinition of the responsibilities of the Citizens' Counsel, as a national mechanism for investigating arbitrariness in the officials of the Hellenic Police, the Port Authority and the Coast Guard and the Firehouse, as well as of the detention officers, as well as the fact that it will be able to carry out investigations independently of any parallel development of criminal proceedings or proceedings, as provided for in Article 188, is considered to be positive of course. I referred earlier to cases of transferring State obligations. Another such example is the ‘opportunity’ to OTAs A' and B grades to operate supervised sites, which can also operate in the form of mobile units, but no reference to their funding, as is made in Article 189. In conclusion, we believe that this draft law will create a pharanonic dimension and responsibilities General Secretariat for Civil Protection without the necessary safety barriers to prevent and resolve all the overlapping that is, with mathematical accuracy, surely will arise. At the same time, we believe that there should be provision for dealing with problems that may be caused by a sharp snowfall, because we live in a country where the possibility of a sharpest snowfall is certainly greater than a cyberattack, without that means that we should not be shielded and prepared, so that we can deal with both phenomena. That is why we are once again seeing the privatisation and concession of state structures, services and responsibilities to voluntary organisations and NGOs, with the State being exempted from fundamental obligations on the protection of life, the infrastructure of the country, the natural environment, which are transferred to the private initiative, with the area of civil protection being left to voluntary action. It is common knowledge that the existing civil protection structures, at central and regional level, have shown gaps, ambiguities, overlaps of competences, and have seen conflicts between those responsible and, finally, everyone denying their responsibilities. Memorials from the recent natural disasters are fresh, which have caused us to mourn hundreds of victims, to witness total destruction of property and natural environments. Studies by specialist scientists have demonstrated huge functional weaknesses at the final and operational level, notably in the dissemination and coordination of stakeholders in risk prevention and risk management issues. The operational and administrative upgrading of the General Secretariat of Civil Protection through the establishment of a National Risk Management and Risk Management Mechanism is therefore necessary in order to achieve, on the one hand, the increased operational effectiveness required and, on the other, to emphasise the more effective defence of human life, property, natural wealth and our cultural heritage. In addition, the creation of this mechanism will fully clarify the responsibilities and tasks, eliminate bureaucracy and detachment of responsibilities and minimise the time-consuming decision-making and action-making processes at strategic, operational and regular level. The analysis of risks and threats is established, through the preparation of the National Civil Protection Plan, both for the implementation of emergency plans and for the management of their consequences. Scientific knowledge, the findings of applied research and infrastructure innovation, the applications and tools for information and communication, through the adoption of an administrative model, which is fully in line with technological and scientific developments. A system of civil protection volunteering, with emphasis on education, based on good organisation, functionality and effectiveness, is established to ensure optimal and safe operational action. An NSRP Final Structure shall be established in order to make more efficient use of available national and European resources in civil protection actions. National Risk Database, Threats, Loss and Disasters is created. A Standing Scientific Council on Civil Protection is hereby established. The operation of the European Forest Fires Centre begins. A Operational Risk Prevention and Management Fund and a Crisis Management Study Centre is hereby established. With regard to the Fire Corps, the proposed draft law proceeds to its more rational reorganisation, based on two structural factors, firstly, on strengthening administrative and operational efficiency and secondly on rational, more efficient and more beneficial management of resources and human resources, so that the Fire Corps is the most modern and worthwhile civil protection structure, which provides timely and efficient services to citizens and the country. Therefore, new decentralised regional fire-fighting services are being established with specific local competence, with administrative and operational autonomy. The National School for Crisis Management and Risk Management is set up, including the Fire Academy, with a view to obtaining legal personality, obtaining rights to exploit resources and partnerships, training for fire officers in an internationally upgraded training system. The legal system is reformed to regulate issues relating to hierarchy, service development, evaluation, changes, movements, transfers and secondments to eliminate issues of transparency and controlled reliability. In the current Community directive on floods, therefore, the dangerous concept of cost-benefit is openly formulated, which must be taken into account in the design of flooding projects. ParlaMint-GR_2020-02-04-S1-commons.u109	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, the amendment which is being debated today deals with urgent matters from the re-established Ministry of Migration and Asylum. It will immediately follow a bill to further speed up the asylum procedure and the returns of those who are not entitled to international protection, which is key to the decongestion of the islands. Today five Aegean islands are raising the burden of Europe, of the migratory crisis. We stress the need to further strengthen border guarding, to set up control centres instead of open structures currently operating within urban tissues and to control action by NGOs. Yesterday we experienced an unacceptable situation in Mytilini, a situation which we will face with plan and determination. I stress that the state's primary obligation is the security of its citizens. Consequently, these unpleasant events confirm the need to speed up the asylum procedure, returns to those who are not entitled to asylum and the operation of closed control centres. It is also obvious that infringements are inconsistent with asylum applications, in accordance with the asylum procedure. We immediately strengthened security measures and, as I said yesterday, we are investigating the possibility of inciting such reactions. Today's amendment is to strengthen the necessary legislative framework. The first article defines the distinct responsibilities of the National Coordinator who is fully responsible for the organisation, management and coordination and control of the Single Border Surveillance Body. Improving logistical infrastructure, strengthening human resources and the optimal cooperation between the Armed Forces and the Security Corps clearly sends out the message that the country has borders and is effectively defending them against past ideas. With Article 2, we establish the register of members of non-governmental organisations and thus tighten up the control framework. This is a new register of natural persons of NGOs, members, officials and associates working on Greek territory. With this process we offer the public administration and the competent authorities the institutional knowledge necessary for the functioning of the Greek and foreign NGOs, which will no longer be impersonal. I have to tell you that there are, of course, organisations which have worked exemplarily, which have contributed to creating sustainable conditions in this migratory crisis. However, there are others that we do not know in depth about their action and should be certified and approved, so that they continue to have access to the provision of services. The new provision allows us to delete from the register organisations and persons who do not fulfil the conditions for cooperation. We shall also proceed immediately to the placement of a special secretary at the Ministry of Immigration and Asylum in order to effectively supervise the new measure. Article 3 provides for the assent of the Minister for Migration and Asylum for the transfer and secondment of officials of the Ministry, a provision necessary for the better management of human resources, and the management flexibility needed to respond effectively to the ongoing emerging challenges. Article 4 provides for the accelerated authorisation of new pre-regulatory centres necessary for the return policy of those who are not entitled to international protection. Article 5 gives the National Solidarity Centre the flexibility needed to organise actions related to the protection and supervision of unaccompanied minors. Article 6 provides for the financial relief by means of a special grant to the municipalities that raise the burden of hosting refugees and migrants. To this end, we are financing EUR 25 000 000 to cover the cost of the impact of local services and infrastructure. The state comes and pays municipal fees for those who live on the border of the municipalities. We set the level of monthly compensation per person and predict further increases in cases of overconcentration, which will unfortunately also change, but is currently happening in the Aegean islands. And we immediately grant for 2019 retrospectively EUR 150 for each resident in 2019 to hosting units and we will do that in February. We are promoting arrangements that build a sound platform of confidence between the central government and local government, which are called upon in everyday life to tackle the first line of this great crisis. It is a move of respect, it is a responsibility of the state to the municipalities, and I am sure it will act as a precursor of consensus for the deep areas that are needed. With Articles 7 and 8 we are directly launching within a month the creation of a solidarity fund with a budget also of EUR 25 000 000, with the aim of financing actions to further support local communities charged with cohabiting with host units within their administrative limits. Ladies and gentlemen, our islands have lifted a disproportionate weight for five years and the current situation cannot continue. We're changing politics. Our first priority is the reduction of border guarding flows, the acceleration of asylum, the creation of closed structures, the return of those who are not entitled to international protection. Our second priority is to support local societies that raise the burden of the crisis. We are working on the night with a plan and determination. We'll succeed. Thank you.

It improves training and training of the members of the Fire Corps with the incorporation of the Fire Academy into the new National Crisis Management and Destruction School. Substantial steps are also being taken to better education, operational involvement and the protection of volunteer fire extinguishers. Clear mechanisms and procedures are provided for the preventive removal of citizens, namely their movement from an area where a catastrophic event is likely to occur and before it occurs. With the National Risk and Threat Database, all those elements that may warn of a risk are recorded and the guidelines on which action should be taken and when. The brain of the management mechanism is becoming more operational at the time of the crisis, the National Business Coordinating Centre. ParlaMint-GR_2020-02-04-S1-commons.u150	Thank you, Madam President. Minister, ladies and gentlemen, in general terms, the existing crisis management and response system has failed. It places particular emphasis on the prevention and preparation of the entire state apparatus, with a view to avoiding extreme disasters and, in any case, to overcome them in the best way possible. The National Mechanism for Crisis Management and Risk Management is an ambitious project that has as a core the overall upgrade of the General Secretariat of Civil Protection and develops on four axes: prevention, preparedness, response and rehabilitation. The aim of the entire project is to better coordinate between the competent bodies and to record clear responsibilities for all those involved, so that there is no observance of responsibilities. The coordinating bodies shall be developed at central, regional and local level. One of the priorities of the new effort is the use of research, innovation, technology through the creation for the first time of a register of experts, but also of a permanent scientific council, so that there is direct cooperation with experts. It is also of paramount importance that the National Risk and Threat Database is established, as the potential risks are specified in detail and, in particular, specific responses to the mechanism are identified, depending on the specific problem. Particular care is taken to protect citizens, thanks to the emergency plans, which - especially at regional and municipal level - include clear instructions on organised evacuation, as well as alternative routes for the removal of citizens, ways of transporting them and informing the public. The National School for Crisis Management and Risk Management undertakes theoretical and practical training, as well as the retraining of the involved. Critical is also the significant upgrade of the Fire Academy, which becomes a legal person. A real section of the bill is the arrangements for volunteers. To date, the legal framework for voluntary organisations, which are assisting civil protection, has been dubious and incomplete. The training of volunteers is now regulated, the categorisation of voluntary organisations and a specific procedure is provided for in order to mobilise, where necessary, as well as the possibility of receiving support in materials and instruments. Finally, it is clear for the first time that volunteers are able to drive and operate fire-fighting vehicles and machinery so that these capabilities are not exploited. The provisions of the bill concerning the Fire Corps aspire to modernise it administrative and operationally and improve its organisational structures, while solving many time problems. In this direction, the responsibilities of regional administrations are increased in order to achieve decentralisation in the areas of organisation. At the same time, special duties are provided for in order to make full use of those employees who are legal, economists and engineers. Enlargement is being made of bodies that can operate as supervised drug use sites and are now taking the role of local government bodies. The program "PESSEAS", which worked for a short period of time in 2013, showed that part of the users trusted it and at least a hundred people were saved from overdose. The example of other European countries also shows us that we can have long-term benefits from these sites, of supervised use, but also from mobile units that will have that role. Studies indicate that there is evidence of death reductions, a reduction in the incidence of HIV, hepatitis C and B, and inhibition in areas where such areas exist. It is time to treat drug users as what they really are, that is, as patients, to provide medical care, not to marginalize them and gradually to feel the necessary security and confidence in the state mechanisms to take the next step and join therapeutic programmes. Today's bill is a dynamic attempt to create a direct and modern national mechanism for managing natural and man-made disasters. Most experts on civil protection issues welcomed it positively. The implementation of the new mechanism ensures a holistic and sound response to crises so that human lives are not jeopardised in the future. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-02-04-S1-commons.u174	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, this draft law by the Ministry of Civil Protection seeks to create a new, modern and operational institutional framework for crisis management and risk management. The results of the claws and malfunctions were evident during the previous parliamentary term. Who forgets the unspeakable tragedy in Attica of 2018, but also the vaterlo that prevailed on political and operational planning? We must, Minister, and you are right to do so, move on to an institutional and innovative organisational reform. That's what this draft law does. With its areas and improvements, the structure of civil protection is better placed on the ground, as well as its main operational arm, which is the Fire Corps. The General Secretariat of Civil Protection shall be upgraded administratively and operationally. A national mechanism of overall, vertical management, which guarantees a timely and effective response to each emergency situation shall be established. Central and regional coordination structures and bodies shall be established, with fully clarified responsibilities and tasks for all levels of administration. The aim is to ensure speed in reaction and decision-making times. It seeks to eliminate bureaucracy and eliminate the diffusion status and, ultimately, to withdraw responsibility. It is planned to establish a National Risk and Threat Database in order to achieve risk and threat analysis. The National Mechanism for Crisis Management and Risk Management is established in the standards of other countries, which will make use of applied research and IT and communication tools. A permanent Civil Protection Scientific Board is hereby established, which is also planned to operate as the European Centre for Forestry. At the same time, staffing will also be strengthened. First of all, we refer to the Fire Corps, which is actually being upgraded through the bill, satisfying the main demands of its officials. First of all, lifelong training of firefighting officials is a priority of the current government. The Fire Academy, under the umbrella of the National Crisis and Risk Management School, is being upgraded. The School of Firefighters is also being upgraded, gaining academic status. Excellence is being rewarded by the invasion of the first five of the School of Firefighters at the School of Officers. Fire extinguishers who hold a licence or a postgraduate degree shall be upgraded by establishing a provision for staff of special duties, legal, economists, engineers and other professions or studies. The upgrading of the operation of the Fire Corps passes through a pioneering system of evaluation of the executives of all degrees, through a crystal and aptitude process. The same is true of the transfers for which a new moleculation-based transfer regulation is envisaged for all. Finally, apart from permanent ones, there are five-year obligation issues in the bill. In particular, Article 180 deletes the concept of potential renewal and defines that it is explicitly renewed under the terms and conditions set out in paragraph 7 of that Article. In this way an important request is made by the Association of Firefighters of Macedonia - Thrace and North Aegean, whose board of directors has repeatedly visited my political office in Alexandroupolis. This is because it was not constitutionally guaranteed and it was acceptable that their contracts should be extended until retirement. In addition, new seasonal staff may be recruited, up to six months each year and up to five anti-nuclear periods. This means that there will be a mechanism in place at the beginning of the next anti-missile period instead of last year's procedure, which you will recall that the notice was made on 6 May and expired on 15 May. Finally, in connection with the upgrading of firefighting services this year, the Government plans to recruit a thousand five hundred seasonal fire extinguishers. To conclude, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to inform you that this bill is innovative. I can tell you that it is in line with the general regulation that applies to the operation of the Greek Police. All that you will be voting in favour has already been implemented and, indeed, we also have a success in terms of operation. Because it exploits our experience as a country, as I said, through the Greek Police and incorporates good practices of previous laws, but also of other countries. It adapts civil protection to modern climatic, scientific and technological data, while at the same time multipliering the role of the Fire Corps and the voluntary movement. Ladies and gentlemen, natural disasters will continue to exist and occur in our country, such as fires, earthquakes, floods and anything else that can happen in a country. It is an opportunity for you, who have experienced the great catastrophe in Attica in 2018, to vote in favour of this law, because history will record you in the brave God. Otherwise, you will apologize for what happened in Attica. We are all witnessing these sad and tragic events. Thank you for your attention.

The key to the success of the mechanism is its vertical structure, in which the responsibilities and tasks of all stakeholders are fully clarified. It is described in detail who does what, when it does and of course who is responsible for any action or omission. The structure includes a central coordinator, thirteen regional coordinators and thirteen regional coordination centres.
