ParlaMint-GR_2020-02-04-S1-commons.u203	Ladies and gentlemen, this bill proposed to the vote by the Ministry of Civil Protection is trying to put a delicate and critical political issue on solid foundations, which is no longer a political issue of civil protection. This need came because of climate change and other emerging threats that affect all countries without exception but in a non-formal way. Several scientific studies on climate change show that its impact on the Mediterranean countries will be comparatively stronger, with serious economic effects, on employment, on growth, on incomes, on agriculture, on tourism, on the spread of diseases, on productivity, on migratory waves, on mortality and on other sectors. A recent example of the current climate change in Greece is the floods in West Attica in 2017, which showed not only the size of the problem but also the lack of the state mechanism. Another case of state inertia and non-coordination of the competent authorities is the fires in Eye and the tragic consequences with the victims we have mourned. Therefore, the possibility of anticipating and suppressing these risks that are substantially threatening human life and property is intensified by coordinated state actions. The primary civil protection system is unable to offer sufficient solutions in this direction because of its antiquated nature and its significant deficiencies. In the light of these circumstances, the draft law defines the establishment of the National Risk Management and Risk Management Mechanism supervised by the General Secretariat of Civil Protection and distinguished for its single character and its vertical organisation. Within this institutional framework, the General Secretariat for Civil Protection has a prominent role and further improves its responsibilities so that it can cope with any emergency situations caused by natural or technological disasters and threats in good time. At the same time, the supportive and operational structures of the national mechanism are clearly defined and the structural restructuring of the Firehouse is attempted so that it is more effective in its operation while at the same time fulfilling its operational role in the exercise of civil protection. Indicatively, just to mention that the Firehouse is now part of the building of the National Crisis Management and Management School of Risks so as to acquire legal personality and thus become a body for the use of resources and partnerships at national and supranational level, while at the same time the Firefighting School of the Academy is being supreme. The most important interventions for changes to this draft law in the area of civil protection are as follows. A national plan for civil protection of three years shall be established, drawn up at national and regional level. First, the emergency management frameworks for which the national mechanism is operationally and functionally supported shall be specified. Secondly, the coordinating civil protection bodies. The existing civil protection coordination body as the highest operational body and the regional bodies and local coordination bodies, respectively. Thirteen regional operational civil protection centres are recommended and their responsibilities are defined and how they should be organised and operated. The support services of the national mechanism and their responsibilities shall be clearly identified, the manner and the staffing process. Provision is made for the establishment in the national mechanism of a national database of risks, threats and losses, at national level. The general planning for emergency response and impact management and other actions shall be carried out and implemented. Also, other important provisions appearing in this bill are the redefinition of the responsibilities of the Citizens' Counsellor as a national mechanism to investigate arbitrariness in the officials of the Greek Police, the Port Authority, the Hellenic Coast Guard, the Firehouse and the employees of detention stores. The draft law also upgrades the voluntary dimension of civil protection. Volunteers' activation is a key issue on the government's political agenda, and volunteer participation in the exercise of civil protection can only be of added value. Unfortunately, Greece was also a reality in this area of public policy. The training of civil protection volunteers is of paramount importance, which is prerequisite for obtaining and maintaining the status of civil protection volunteer and is provided by the School of Volunteers and Volunteers of the Academy of Civil Protection. There is a clear categorisation of voluntary organisations according to possibilities of assistance and support to state bodies. A clear procedure and conditions for mobilisation and operational involvement of volunteers shall be laid down. Arrangements allowing aid to materials and instruments of voluntary organisations shall be introduced. In conclusion, the overall assessment of the bill could only be positive and its contribution is triple. First of all, the Government of the New Democrat Republic strongly shields the constitutionally established goods of life, health, property, the protection of the natural environment and cultural heritage against any threat and possible threat. This option is an unnecessarily high priority of the present government, because it always helps to develop and promote citizens. Secondly, it is attempting to decentralise the management of natural and other disasters with the spearhead of the thirteen regional civil protection centres and the respective services of the municipalities. And thirdly, the spirit of volunteering is being strengthened in our country, through the provision of quality civil protection services by citizens themselves, volunteers, for citizens. Finally, I would like to make a point about the five-year obligation firefighters, Mr Minister, from the city-based city, unless he wishes to do so. Minister, ladies and gentlemen, the rapid increase in the movement of people and goods in the national provincial network of the Serres Prefecture, in the last decade, with the two main roads of the Ennatian road - vertical axis, which is a portal with the Balkans and the Egnatia road to the south - the important gas transmission hub - passes through the county of all the pipelines by the east - the huge forest volume, with the huge space of responsibility due to distances, makes the need to upgrade the fire service in the county. The data have changed and it is necessary to take all necessary measures to ensure that the services of the firefighting industry are effective for the safety of forest wealth and of the citizen. We propose that Serres Fire Service upgrade to Class I Firehouse, with Nigrita Fire Scale being administratively owned by it and the financing of a new building on an existing plot of the Firehouse, the upgrading of the Ferrokastro Fire Stage to Class IV Fire Stage with the Ano Poros Fire Climb under its command and the upgrading of the Fire Climbs at Class IV Fire Stage with the New Zichnis Fire Climb to be administratively owned by it. Also, the establishment of a national stadium fire extinguisher, on the Egnatia street, at the New Corfu node. I believe that in addition to the major institutional changes made by this bill, we must also bend to these individual issues, because, as I said before, the data have changed, to protect the environment and citizens from potential disasters by climate change and other threats. Thank you.

There is no room for micropolitical and senseless opposition here. No government seeks to jeopardise the lives of citizens. Nevertheless, it is in the hands of the state, but it is also an obligation at the same time to take the necessary preventive measures in time so that our country does not relive such unspeakable tragedies. This draft law comes to implement another commitment by the Mitsotakis Government, with two main objectives. The first objective is to reorganise the great institution of Civil Protection, as well as its operational arm, which is the Fire Corps. Objective 2, to correct any operational weaknesses and structural contagions, particularly at the level of coordination of stakeholders. We are, of course, living in a time of climate change. The changing climatic conditions, which are linked to the major natural disasters in recent years, cause so much concern and debate throughout the world that the existing institutional framework for the organisation and functioning of the Civil Protection Secretariat is now becoming obsolete, incomplete and ineffective, as has been demonstrated in practice. Today we seek the administrative and operational upgrading of the General Secretariat of Civil Protection and through this upgrade will come better efficiency, more effective protection of human life, but also of property and, of course, of the greatest perhaps goods, beyond human life, which is the natural wealth of our country and our cultural heritage. Furthermore, until now there has been no single national mechanism for vertical management holistic treatment, from the prevention phase to the restoration phase of any damage arising from natural disasters. On the contrary, there was a fragmentation, a dangerous, as it has proved, on every occasion, overlapping and worst, a conflict of competence between civil protection bodies at national level, at local level, at regional level, a complete confusion, as has emerged in some cases, with dramatic consequences. With this draft law we are moving towards the establishment of a National Risk Management and Risk Management Mechanism, the good standards of the American FEMA and Russian EMERCOM. We are moving towards a vertical structure, with separate coordination bodies, for all levels of administration and all stakeholders. There is now a complete clarification of responsibilities, a clarification of tasks, so that in the event of a crisis everyone knows exactly what to do and from which retreat it must fight its own battle. That was the problem over the years, along with, of course, the inability to prevent. We are moving towards shortening time-consuming crisis decision-making and taking action at strategic, operational and tactical level. We are adopting a modern administrative model, in line with current technological and scientific developments. The Standing Scientific Committee on Civil Protection is being set up, and in this way, we are making progress in ensuring, at the same time, those tools that will help to make use of the scientific opinion, the findings of applied research and technology, innovation, information and communication. With the adoption of the bill, start and operate the European Forest Fires Centre, with the aim of fulfilling this mission and this crisis, which every year we have to pass on to our forests with summer fires, in an ever-changing environment, as has been said by many colleagues, because of climate change. At the same time, the National Database of Risks, Threats and Disasters is being created. Thus, the existing threats, risks and the consequences of any disasters are directly recorded. Accelerating the operational upgrading of emergency plans and the management of consequences through specialisation, depending on the specificities of the risk to be addressed, the specificities of the area threatened by that risk, and of course, depending on the means available to each other. Voluntary organisations - a very important aspect of the bill - with logistical infrastructure, by all means and clearly categorised. A framework is established for the operational involvement and integration of volunteers in the National Civil Protection Mechanism. We move on to the establishment of a National Coordinating Centre for Operations and Crisis Management and the individual units that will coordinate and assist this project. I shall refer only to the unit of the emergency number, 112, which is finally working. The National School for Crisis Management and Risk Management will have administrative and financial autonomy, with the spear's edge the Fire Academy. Only in this way, with education, will the culture of social responsibility and of supply be cultivated, which must be distinguished by bodies which are the operational arm, but also by volunteers who are involved. At this point I would like, Minister, to address a number of issues relating to the Fire Corps. We've all recorded labels made by firefighters, and I think it's good to hear them. I shall put forward a number of points for the Minutes, which I consider to be just, from the point of view of the recognition of the profession as dangerous and unhealthy, to the point of view of building infrastructure. Five-year firefighters are finally asking for their other colleagues to be equated at all levels, not just simulation but also wages. I share this view because there cannot be two categories of fire extinguishers within the same House. The issues that have been raised in your mind, Minister, which relate to the renewal of the infrastructure fleet, even of the buildings, are also very important, because the more outdated is equipment, instruments and vehicles, the greater the risks for fire-fighting themselves, apart from being able to adequately address the danger. In conclusion, ladies and gentlemen, it is understood that this draft law best organizes civil protection at structure level and efficiency. It is time for the Greeks to start feeling that the state is on their side and takes all necessary steps to make everyday life better, but also safer. We have been moving in this direction for the last seven months, so we will continue. I present for the Minutes a series of documents from the Macedonian fire extinguishers' associations - Thrace, North Aegean, Halkidiki. [[At this point, the Member, Mr Dimitrios Kouvellas, presents for the minutes the above mentioned documents, which are in the archives of the Secretariat Department of the Department of Stenography and Practice of the Parliament.]] Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-02-04-S1-commons.u274	Mr Ioannis Bougas, Parliamentary Representative of the New Democracy.
ParlaMint-GR_2020-02-04-S1-commons.u275	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, when discussing a draft law on civil protection one must consider cases of natural disasters in order to determine how the system operates. The most recent example of natural disasters at global level is the unprecedented fires that broke out in Australia and have made an ashes of a total area of 100 thousand square kilometres. The victims of these disasters amount to thirty. In our country, the fire that burned Matty Attica destroyed an area of thirteen square kilometres in an area of about four thousand inhabitants with tragic results the loss of a hundred and two fellow people. The difference between the tragic consequences of the two phenomena is absolutely indicative and reveals poverty in the field of organisation. The reality of crisis management in our country has mainly demonstrated a deficit of coordination both horizontally between the co-responsible ministries and vertically, i.e. between the institutions of the decentralised state and the local government, a deficit of coordination, a deficit of responsibility, and above all a deficit of leadership that led to actions without proper organisation and therefore ineffective, has unfortunately led to unspeakable tragedies. I was very pleased, because the previous speaker, Mr Dritsa, said that we wish the draft law to succeed. I think he has the potential to succeed. I will not respond to what has been said about the exercise of an opposition duty by colleagues in the opposition. I want to highlight the positive aspects of the bill and that is too much. The draft law is national because it fundamentally reorganises the country's civil protection, forms the new General Secretariat for Civil Protection as a single, autonomous, powerful national crisis management and risk management mechanism with new structures, with economic, administrative self-sufficiency and the possibility of direct absorption and use of Community funds. A central objective is the timely and correct response to any kind of natural disaster, to any emergency caused by disasters and threats with vertical structure, as detailed in the provisions of the proposed draft law, so as to determine who is responsible in any case, who decides and who is accountable to who. New structures and coordination bodies at both central and regional level with fully clarified responsibilities. If one looks at the draft law, one finds that the criticism, in particular, of the Officerial Responsibility for vague responsibilities among the competent bodies is unfounded. The clarification of competences is both central and, above all, at local and regional level. A central coordinator, thirteen regional coordinators and thirteen regional coordination centres respectively. In this way, a more rapid response is sought, a elimination of bureaucracy and a prevention of the spread of responsibility, because in such cases there must be the person responsible. To be effective in protecting, ladies and gentlemen, we are focused on preventing the proposed draft law. The National Risk and Threats Database is created as the main prerequisite, as well as an element of the National Civil Protection Design. The aim is to contribute to the specificity of the projects concerned according to the degree of risk, so that the guidelines given by the national mechanism to the bodies and operational bodies are as appropriate as possible. Instruments for achieving the objective, exploiting the experience and findings of applied research and information and communication tools, by creating models and mechanisms for informing and warning citizens. I think that everyone in this Chamber agrees that the existing operational weaknesses and structural squeezes of the system, particularly at the level of coordination of stakeholders on a case-by-case basis, confirm that the current legislative framework is ineffective and fails to meet the current requirements. The Chief of Officership as Prime Minister was, if I'm not mistaken, successful in abolishing the same secretariat. However, following the pressure caused by the tragic manipulations in a major disaster, it finally prepared a piece of legislation which, as has been said, could not be passed on to the National Representation. Finally, today the demonic provisions of the legislation are codified in a basic and coherent text. A completely new holistic philosophy is introduced, as the single disaster management from all natural risks is introduced for the first time in Greece. For example, in the United States this was introduced in 1993 and in France in 2004. Prevention, preparedness, response and rehabilitation shall be incorporated into national disaster risk reduction policy. The proposed draft law provides for the establishment of an Integrated Political Protection Facility as an autonomous structure, part of the NSRP's climate change axis, which will facilitate the short and targeted absorption of money intended for these critical actions. In addition, the Centre for Crisis Management Studies, as well as the Operational Risk Prevention and Management Fund will achieve effective use of national and European resources, through direct funding of studies, work or supplies falling within the responsibility of the General Secretariat for Civil Protection. The new Directorate-General for Economic Affairs strengthens the financial autonomy of the Secretariat-General and therefore offers flexibility in the management and budget maintenance guarantee. An important innovation is also the introduction of a fast track for the rapid licensing and execution of emergency projects, preventive interventions and/or emergency rehabilitation interventions of any affected persons, which are assessed first by the Risk Assessment Committee to avoid timely bureaucracy and thus to ensure the absorption of funds available. However, the point that is absolutely crucial is the provisions on the intellectual potential of civil protection, for which unfortunately they have been heard in the Chamber. The bill, ladies and gentlemen, has the starting point for volunteers, replacing their non-functional regulatory framework of action with a grid of operational involvement provisions, with emphasis on protection, education and clear categorisation of voluntary organisations. It is also envisaged that voluntary associations can be strengthened by material means so that society can be actively involved in civil protection. The draft law also contains provisions on the chief operational arm of civil protection, the Fire Corps. The Fire Corps is being upgraded. It finally takes the place that is appropriate among the guarantors of the protection of the life and property of the Greeks. To this end, the Central Crisis and Risk Management School, structured in the Fire Academy, the Civil Protection Academy and the Centre for Special Training, is to be set up. The upgrading of the operation of the Fire Corps is classified by a pioneering system of evaluation of the officers, as well as the other personnel of the House through a rated table of enhanced transparency and integrity, abolishing any inequalities based on measurable professional skills. Similarly, it changes the status of promotions and transfers. Special scientists of the Fire Corps are now exploited, executives are trained and introduced through pan-Hellenic examinations. I want to come to another matter now. There has been a lot of talk in the debate on the draft law in the committee responsible, but also before the plenary on sites for supervised use, where local government will now be involved. This is also the innovation of the bill, the involvement of local government in these already existing areas. In this respect, there are spaces from qualified professionals, i.e. healthcare facilities, where substances can be used safely and in particular by clean means. The aim is firstly to protect public health by preventing the transmission of infectious diseases and the health of the active users by preventing, but also in time, intervention to deal with overdose in order to prepare for their inclusion in treatment programmes. The aim is also to eliminate, or even to reduce, the phenomena that cause fear to citizens and unfortunately tarnish the image of cities. Ladies and gentlemen, I would remind everyone of the picture that until recently existed in the center of Athens, and I refer specifically to the entrance of the Law School from Marseille Street, where students, tourists, unsuspecting citizens of transit, as well as shop customers, were literally passing by the syringes from users who ratted each other's body. Such scenes we want on the urban web? That's the kind of scene we want in our cities? Do we want users using means, which have been used without any protection and without any supervision? So this is what we are trying to finish, because the legal regime, if I'm not mistaken, goes back to 2013. Interventions have been made by law of SYRIZA, but also ministerial decisions by the Minister of Health. Ladies and gentlemen, because time is running out, I would like to say two more words. Civic Opposition prejudges the bill, which we are called upon to vote, as a malfunctioning and proposed structure of the General Secretariat as a demonic one. This formal reproach - because I have not heard any other arguments against the bill - demonstrates the lack of substantial arguments. The draft law brings a series of substantial changes that will contribute to a functional, coherent and effective civil protection system. This is a great effort by the Government for the radical reform of the issues regulated by the draft law, for which it is worth trying to congratulate the ministers and, in particular, the Minister for Civil Protection. Thank you for your attention.

The Government had at its disposal a proposal for a law by the SYRIZA Government, which laid down, in general, a mechanism of civil protection that was modern, comprehensive, effective and in line with the international standards currently in force. This bill had been put in long consultation with all stakeholders, as well as with the scientific community. Sadly, however, instead of relying on it and improving it - all things are better - you have served in a dangerous exercise of amateurism. That, Mr General, was the question I asked you in the Committee on Regional Policy, namely what you are changing from the proposal for a law of the SYRIZA. You replied to me in general that there were no specific responsibilities in the operational mechanism pyramid. In my opinion, here too, you are going to apply the concept of a state with over-concentration of powers and powers. So, let us consider what exactly you are doing because, in the condolence you are presenting to us, you are doing exactly the opposite. Your bill introduces incorrect additions, carries out surgical removals, thus deorganising the crisis and risk management mechanism and the general concern of the involved operators and citizens. But I will concentrate my comments on the role and degraded position of the bill in local government, especially the first degree, on the basis of my experience as Mayor of Livadeon from 2014 to 2019. Ladies and gentlemen, we all agree - at least in our speeches - that municipalities are the first obstacle to dealing with emergencies, since they are the first and direct witnesses of all the risks and crises. The experience of disasters so far shows a number of actions that the municipalities were asked to take, and which in many cases exceeded the institutionally defined scope of their activity. Therefore, the absence of the first degree of self-government from the national planning of civil protection, Article 9(4), is very serious omission. Article 23 of the bill describes the role of Municipal Civil Protection Independents. The article is about similar to Article 21 of the SYRIZA's proposal for a law, but with one significant difference. Three series have been removed which negate the competence of paragraph (b), i.e. the municipality's competence to organise and ensure the smooth functioning of the City's Civil Protection Coordination Centre. This competence is accorded to the Regional Civil Protection Coordination Bodies, POPS, and to all phases, from prevention to rehabilitation. This regulation, which provides for the institutions of the regions to develop control and hierarchy relations with the municipalities, is dysfunctional and unconstitutional, rather, ladies and gentlemen, since it violates the autonomy of the local government bodies, which is, of course, pointed out by both the CEDEFOP and the EIC respectively. But let me not make my point anymore. I prefer to focus on the views of the CEDEFOP and the EIA, which are all against you. The CEDEFOP, which finds the draft law daunty and possibly dysfunctional, points out the absolute absence of reference to the role and integration of the authorities of the first degree in the design. It also stresses that special reference should be made to making it clear that measures and actions of a local and/or trans-popular nature are designed and implemented exclusively by primary authorities and calls for the safeguarding of all institutionalised resources, as well as for the conversion of the RBRD into a civil protection system, with more than doubled its resources. It also proposes responsibility for the preventive removal of citizens to have it operationally rather than what is currently in place and reproduced in your bill. Why, does it not make sense not to prosecute local mayors when others have the general command of operational responsibility? Finally, the JRC formulates many specific proposals to address different risks, precisely because municipalities are a key involved institution. Moreover, the Greek Committee of the Regions agrees that the coordination of civil protection at local level should have it by the Departments of Civil Protection. The regulations on OTAs are part of the ambiguities and contradictions of the draft law, in which we can add the increased powers of retired Regional Policy Coordinators, ambiguity with regard to their role and responsibilities in relation to present and service staff, but also the additional cost of remuneration and liability benefits, the introduction of secret confidential funds for direct assignments and much more emphasised by consultation, as well as today's debate. Ladies and gentlemen, the risks of natural phenomena are in front of us and do not allow amateurism, with stitches and ambiguities, like those of the bill. Former Civil Protection Secretary, Yiannis Tafyllis, a high-level expert, examined the implementation of the mechanism provided for by your bill in the tragic disaster in Matti and proves the complete inability to deal with the disaster. Really, why didn't you attempt your model simulations? All of this is of particular concern to us for the future of emergency needs and crises in our country. It is, however, the government's preoccupation in the laws it legislates and the bankruptcy of the state's staff, and, unfortunately, we predict that you will come back many times in this law with corrective amendments, as they stand. But I don't know if the bill we're discussing is corrected because it's in spirit and planning. In the case of civil protection, ladies and gentlemen, the consequences are not generally related to the deterioration of the social conditions of our lives. They concern the protection of the environment, the protection of the property, the goods of citizens, and the protection of human life. Thank you.

Here we are negotiating an issue that is in the hard core of the state. If there is an issue that falls within the competence and responsibility of the hard core of the public sphere, public power is security in the broad sense and the issue of civil protection is security. It is the security of the citizen, of society, of the country, of the country's infrastructure, of private property from natural disasters, of the crises that are erupting, which are becoming more and more dangerous, because it changes the climate of the planet. The Australian case is not a short-term case, a summer in Australia where we have major fires. It is precisely the outbreak of climate change, which takes the dimensions of a storm and which destroyed a large part of Australia's forest vegetation, took human lives, destroyed property and significantly affected the GDP of a very large and rich country, such as Australia. The same is true of a series of crises and disasters taking place in the United States, in Europe with floods and so on. Here, therefore, is a task of policy to formulate, organise policies for civil protection, crisis management and disaster response. That is the aim of our conversation today. What's the problem so far? How do I understand the problem so far? What's the matter? Why are we coming today to legislate? The problem is that unfortunately there are disguised responsibilities. There are difficulties in reaching agreement across the horizontal and vertical borders. There are horizontal and vertical difficulties in being able to cooperate, so that all too often results in a lack of efficiency. I am not going to look at the Eye right now, just to mention it as a symptom, because there are many things in crisis management and disaster response. I would like to point out that the vote includes the principle of the bill, a hundred and eighty-nine articles, an amendment, a farewell article and the whole bill. Each time on the screen they appear as four articles for vote. To vote and the rest, you need to roll the touch screen. At the top right of the screen the number of articles left to vote is shown each time. And, of course, you must make sure that you have passed all the articles, the amendment, as well as the farewell article and the whole bill. After your vote has been registered, you may check it or even revise it until the end of the vote. I would ask that the electronic voting system be opened.
